five-sample benchmark reported 0.84× NYR/C, 1.45× LLVM/C and 0.94× NYC/C; the float
ABI fixture and ORC FFT passed.

`native-oracle` reported `NOISY` and has no kernel timer. Its LLVM IR contains the
literal `832040`, not a Fibonacci loop, and the assembly formats and prints it, so
the large ratio is not evidence of a slow Fibonacci loop. The integer-only AOT
executable still has no libm dependency.

### collision, bignum, and repeat diagnostics

`cd`: NYR already hoists the outer `px[i]`/`py[i]` reads; in the inner loop LLVM IR and
NYIR still express signed absolute differences and conditional hit counting while
Clang emits packed compares and register count accumulation. Folding the two NYR
short-circuit guards into an eager scalar `and` raised instructions 100.47 M→136.21 M
and median time 7.15 ms→9.25 ms across five pinned samples; reverted — the packed
reduction, not scalar speculation, is the opportunity. Every run returned `387`.

`bigint-oracle`: assembly and sampling put the cost in object allocation, pointer
membership, limb decoding and multi-limb division; GMP reuses destination objects.
A split hit/miss generation added 4.08 M instructions to the 165.34 M baseline with
no wall-time gain, skipping repeated compare normalization and splitting carry/borrow
loops left instructions and cycles essentially flat, and deferring limb decode output
stores retired 166.21 M and slowed all seven pinned samples. All three returned
`1968365` and were reverted.

`repeat`: LLVM IR calls `rt_str_repeat_raw`; sampled AOT assembly spends most kernel
cycles in libc's AVX `memcpy`, not checked element access or extra zeroing. In
isolation the unchanged fixture ran within 2× (NYR 1.12×, LLVM 1.27×, NYC 1.48×); the
full parallel run's higher ratios have not been cleared in a complete rerun.

### NYC PBKDF2 low-word encoding

Samples put 89% of NYC's retired instructions in SHA-256 compression; NYIR had already found the 32-bit rotates and masks. The encoder now omits the extra
zero-extension after a known nonzero immediate rotate and lowers an exact low-word mask on the accumulator path to a register zero-extension instead of an AND
from a stack slot. Dynamic rotate counts keep the explicit zero-extension. Five pinned, interleaved samples: 60.57 M → 58.26 M retired instructions, median
9.88 ms → 9.52 ms. Both PBKDF2 fixtures returned `2991` and the x86 masked rotate oracle passed; Clang took 1.17 ms in separate pinned samples. Scope: stack
traffic in the compression loop remains open.

### signed constant divisors in machine IR

`qrsyndrome` had literal divisors 200 and 255 in NYIR, yet its object used three `idiv` sites because register lowering obscured those constants. x86-64
machine IR now keeps signed constant divisors as immediate operands, letting the encoder select its reciprocal multiply-and-shift sequences. The machine route
accepted the shape and the object contained no `idiv`. Five pinned, interleaved samples of the unchanged fixture returned `1161080`: NYC median kernel time
16.85 → 10.84 ms, cycles 48.21 M → 30.45 M, retired instructions 56.15 M → 75.82 M (more, cheaper instructions). Clang took 5.23 ms in the same set. Signed
magic, dependent divmod, and power-of-two division fixtures passed all ten variants.

### rounding under a nonnegative clamp

The NYC LBM inner loop divided by eight with a sign mask, bias add, and arithmetic shift immediately before clamping negatives to zero; both rounding modes
give the same clamped result. Strength reduction now emits the shift alone when neither the quotient nor the comparison has a consumer outside the clamp. Five
interleaved pinned samples on the unchanged fixture returned `152700`: median 8.96 → 8.26 ms, retired instructions 64.30 M → 57.23 M. The new boundary oracle
checks negative inputs whose truncating quotient is zero and an independently consumed comparison. Scope: Clang vectorizes the stencil; that gap remains.

### post-fix Clang-reference run

Local O3 run with six fixture workers, one warm-up, and three measured runs: all 110 fixtures matched checksums. FFT stayed below 2× on all three routes (LLVM
1.92×), as did `qrsyndrome` (NYC 1.62×). The 2× ceiling stayed open: 20/108 valid NYR, 16/108 LLVM, and 31/109 NYC comparisons missed it.

Largest timed gaps: `cd` 12.66× NYC / 9.05× NYR, `pbkdf2` 8.10× NYC, `bigint-oracle` 7.48× NYR / 6.77× LLVM; `native-oracle` showed 17.98× LLVM but its
Fibonacci work is constant-folded and it has no kernel timer. Emitted `bigint-oracle` code had unused `rt_len`/`rt_value_tag` results in the BigFloat
arithmetic loops, from the compiler-generated length/tag lanes of the dynamic call ABI; ordinary runtime effect annotations remain intact (an unused generated
lane differs from an explicit query). For `page-rank`, both native emitters reload and store the accumulator each iteration while Clang holds it in an XMM
register and stores once; the two array bases swap on the backedge, so sinking the store needs a disjointness proof surviving that swap.

An isolated NYC outer-AVX2 `binary` trial kept checksum `3360874588549334` and cut the five-sample median from 6.21 ms to 3.60 ms (Clang 1.05 ms); emitted
code still had 199 YMM frame-access sites. The trial did not change NYC's default target policy.

### dead dynamic call metadata

ADCE now removes unused compiler-generated length/tag queries after inlining; live metadata and explicit source queries keep their effects. In
`bigint-oracle`, the native AOT entry's static `rt_len`/`rt_value_tag` call sites fell from 29 to 7. Five pinned, interleaved samples returned `1968365`:
median retired instructions 166.21 M → 149.49 M, cycles 83.43 M → 75.35 M, kernel time 43.92 → 40.93 ms. Eleven BigFloat and dynamic ABI fixture variants
passed. Scope: measured after the full run above.

### collision and SHA hot loops

LLVM now gets byte GEPs for typed-buffer accesses with integer-only offsets; its optimized `cd` IR uses a four-lane reduction and x86 code uses
`vpcmpgtq`/`vpsubq`. NYR and NYC use `neg; cmovs` for scalar wrapping absolute values, including `INT64_MIN`. With Clang 23.1.5, the focused `cd` run
(checksum `387`) took 5.84 ms NYR, 1.56 ms LLVM, 9.26 ms NYC, and 0.932 ms Clang; LLVM is 1.67× Clang and NYR/LLVM 3.74×, under the existing 5× fixture limit.
In `pbkdf2`, straight-line inlining no longer splits SHA schedule and round blocks; narrowing drops internal low-word masks only when all uses ignore their
high bits. Five pinned samples on the numeric fixture went from 9.46 ms to 8.66 ms and from 58.26 M to 52.33 M retired instructions. Both NYC fixtures still
returned `2991` and remain above 7× Clang. Scope: collision workloads and benchmark limits were not changed.

### vectorizer rejection histogram

The vectorizer now aggregates reject reasons by source file under `NY_TRACE_VECTORIZE=1` or heuristic tracing. A focused `sha256.nshape` compile emitted six
fixture-source rejects: five `unsupported loop-carried PHI` and one `no canonical +1 induction PHI`. Imported library functions appeared separately,
attributed by their source paths. Traced and untraced builds produced byte-identical ELFs (SHA-256
`94c45beb9cbe8ef5de8fff56bd4d888710576bf49e9663db5575796fad376c7f`). Scope: diagnostic only; no runtime speedup established.

### compile phase timing

Added opt-in `--stats` timing for NY and NYC compilation. One focused run: NY's `sha256.ny` compile reported 2.134 s in the phase timer versus 2.244 s
external process wall time; NYC's 1,428-line `src/code/test/lang/c/translation-unit.c` (181 functions and 69 data symbols) reported 74.975 ms versus 77 ms
external wall time. Scope: one-run instrumentation checks, not comparative performance claims. With and without `--stats`, output ELFs were byte-identical:
NYC SHA-256 `c93a126068814bc1e34d4d8282e59f4711841a367e283a98f5dc4297ef62f174` and NY SHA-256
`fd8544b41404d4a176aa0e2bd201e35799cfc07d977801934d31ce7c7b609b4b`.

### encoded home traffic and wide-call locality

P6b reports encoded `frame_ops` separately from explicit home `mov` stores/loads. For `lbm-relax` `main`, the trace reported 101 frame operations and
`objdump` counted 58 stores and 43 loads through `rbp` (101 total); `fhourstones` `main` reported 27, matching 8 stores and 19 loads. The explicit
`stores=`/`loads=` fields count only the subset emitted as home `mov`s; operations folded into arithmetic appear only in `frame_ops`.

P7 removes the whole-function stack-home fallback for functions with a call of more than six arguments; the call-safety proof still disqualifies a register
range crossing a call. In a fixture with a seven-argument call before a hot loop, the loop went from `force-stack=yes`, `loop-registers=none`, 69 frame
operations to `force-stack=no`, `loop-registers=selected`, 10: 59 fewer operations (about 86%), with both executables returning `45000150027`.

### loop-carried home traffic

The x64 loop-home path keeps eligible carried values in one register across backedges and publishes them when a later non-loop consumer needs the home. On
`binary`, disabling it emitted 33 home stores and 26 loads in `main` (120 `rbp`-relative frame operations); enabled, 1 store and 25 loads (57 frame
operations). Both executables returned `3360874588549334`. The change meets P8's store-reduction gate and cuts loop home traffic without adding loads.

### integer home-fold and edge-sink counters

Added direct encoder counters for integer home folds and two-address ALU commutes. `binary` reported one home fold in `main`; its disassembly has
`sub r14, QWORD PTR [rbp-0x280]`, so the spilled RHS is read by the arithmetic instruction rather than a separate reload. The run returned checksum `3360874588549334`.
No commuted ALU opportunity occurred, and the same trace counted 15 edge-add sinks across compiled functions; none crossed a call, so dynamic
rematerialization is untested.

The P5 caller-first color-order trial left gated code unchanged: `ny_fn_search` stayed at 337 instructions with six pushes and six pops, `binary` `main` at
729 instructions with six pushes and six pops; checksums remained `6010` and `3360874588549334`. The allocation-order edit was reverted because no measured
gate moved; pricing remains open for a more targeted change.

### D1 closings and the callee-save trial

P9, P13, and P15 were retired on measurement rather than implemented. P9 (home store to restore-dominator): P8 already left `binary` `main` at 1 home store
and 25 loads (57 frame operations), so a placement rule cannot reduce a single store or address loads; retired as subsumed by P8. P13 (commute a two-address
form): the transform exists at both audit points, allocator tied-input affinity and encoder in-place RHS swap, and `alu_commutes` fired 0 times across nine
fixtures (`binary`, `lbm-relax`, `fhourstones`, `viterbi`, `crc32`, `sha256`, `hash`, `bitcount`, `mandelbrot`); retired as already implemented and
unexercised. P15 (dynamic remat): rematerialisation is gated at the use site by `mach_segment_at(vreg, cur_inst)` and the address-fold predicate in
`mach_sib_add_mem`, so `folded_known` filters rather than authorises; none of the 15 measured edge-add sinks crosses a call, so the reload-count gate is not
exercisable. Retired.

The P5 trial reordered the loop-home colour scan by marginal push/pop cost: callee colours the base allocation already pays for first, then caller-saved
colours, then unused callee colours. `fhourstones`, `binary`, and `lbm-relax` produced byte-identical executables with and without the ordering (`cmp`), with
`main` at 635/730/793 instructions and 6 pushes and 6 pops each in that build. Availability and range proofs decide the scan, not rank; the edit was reverted.

Current-tree `main` baseline by `objdump -d --no-show-raw-insn` over the body up to the first blank line: 641 (`fhourstones`), 730 (`binary`), 782
(`lbm-relax`), 696 (`viterbi`) instructions; checksums `6010`, `3360874588549334`, `152700`, and `3270530` all hold. The earlier 646 used a wider window and
an intermediate build measured 635; both are superseded.

### Pong phase timing and spill-slot reuse

With the AOT cache disabled, one native x86-64 `-O3 --stats` build of `etc/labs/pong.ny` completed in 13.790 s: 0.613 s parsing, 1.365 s codegen preparation,
3.498 s type validation, 4.877 s codegen, and 7.721 s object emission. Some timers are nested, so these cannot be summed. This resolves the missing timing
measurement; the cold compile still exceeds the 5 s target.

Matched native x86-64 compiles with spill-slot reuse disabled versus enabled gave these stack adjustments in `main`:

| Fixture | Disabled | Enabled | Checksum (both runs) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| binary | 3,544 B | 2,376 B | `3360874588549334` |
| lbm-relax | 3,112 B | 2,824 B | `152700` |
| fhourstones | 2,664 B | 2,504 B | `6010` |

Scope: frame size only. Default enablement still needs a fix for encoder caches keyed by slot offset and checks of the prior failures.

### conditional home publication: carried-run attempt

P1's store half was prototyped as a carried-run rule in `mach_vreg_requires_home_after_def`: skip the def-side publish when the defining segment and every
successor entry segment are marked carried in the same colour, so no consumer can read the home before the run ends. The rule never fired: `cond_home` was 0
on eleven fixtures (`binary`, `fhourstones`, `lbm-relax`, `viterbi`, `binary-trees`, `crc32`, `sha256`, `hash`, `bitcount`, `susan`, `mandelbrot`) and
executables were byte-identical with the rule on and off. The edit was reverted rather than kept behind a flag.

The surviving stores are call-boundary publications, since a value crossing a call cannot stay in a caller-saved colour; loads are argument staging and spill
reloads. On `binary` `main`: 730 instructions, 19 home stores, 34 home loads, 85 `rbp`-relative operands. P1's store side has no work left after P8; its load
side needs the per-block residency condition only P2 supplies.

`NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0` is required for a codegen A/B: with the cache on, both arms return the cached object and counts drift between rebuilds (635 and 641 were
both observed for `fhourstones` `main`). The five-fixture baseline with the cache off, after the revert, is 730/635/793/695/685 instructions with checksums
`3360874588549334`, `6010`, `152700`, `3270530`, and `64912`, all held.

### full benchmark and shared-home follow-up

`./make bench` exited 0 with all configured performance gates passing. Report `bench-20261120-062227-3652437.json` recorded geometric means of 1.35× native/C
and 1.07× LLVM/C among fixtures with a C reference of at least 1 ms; its largest gaps were native-oracle (native/C 10.57×), cd (native/LLVM 3.76×), and pbkdf2
(NYC/C 8.78×). After the benchmark compiler was built, two x86 encoder paths gained guards against forwarding RAX by stack offset alone for shared spill
homes. Fresh native x86-64 `-O3` builds with spill-slot coloring off and on matched checksums on binary, lbm-relax, fhourstones, statemate, and pidigits.
Scope: focused checksums only; default spill-slot coloring and the runtime gaps remain open.

### isolated native-oracle repeat

The full report had 58 comparable route results above 2× C, including 23 with C medians of at least 1 ms; the full list is in the local audit. A one-worker
`native-oracle` repeat gave native 106 µs, LLVM 326 µs, C 29 µs, and NYC 29 µs; native/C fell from 10.57× in the full run to 3.71× in isolation. Scope: still
above 2×, and process overhead is part of a workload computing only one Fibonacci result.

### spill-slot coloring: value-identity tags, default stays off

`NYTRIX_SPILL_HOME_COLOR` folds spilled values' live ranges onto shared home slots: frames shrank, but it crashed `fhourstones` and drifted
`statemate`/`pidigits` when the default, because the sticky-RAX tag named a home offset so a pooled slot could forward a neighbour's value. The tag now
carries a vreg (`mach_rax_tag_hit`, `mach_pending_rax_hit`); a shared slot reuses it only for the same vreg, offset-only sites call `mach_home_off_shared` and
require an unshared slot, and `mach_load_rax_slot`/`mach_store_rax_slot` pass the vreg where the caller knows it.

Measured with `build/release/ny-full`, `-O3 --native-backend x86_64`, `NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0`, `main` from `objdump -d --no-show-raw-insn`:

| fixture | instructions off → on | home stores off → on | frame off → on | checksum |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `binary` | 729 → 822 | 33 → 124 | 0xdd8 → 0x948 | `3360874588549334` |
| `fhourstones` | 634 → 635 | 8 → 8 | 0xa68 → 0x9c8 | `6010` |
| `lbm-relax` | 793 → 795 | 58 → 58 | 0xc28 → 0xb08 | `152700` |
| `viterbi` | 694 → 696 | 23 → 24 | 0xaa8 → 0x9e8 | `3270530` |
| `binary-trees` | 685 → 687 | 2 → 3 | 0xb38 → 0xa38 | `64912` |

Frames shrink 6–33%, but pooling defeats home-store elision on a hot loop: `binary` goes from 33 → 124 stores and 729 → 822 instructions. Smaller frames are
not a win on their own, so the default stays one slot per value and the flag stays opt-in. With the flag on: `ny-test` 3/3 on the former regressions
(`fhourstones`, `statemate`, `pidigits`), `--pattern regalloc --pattern codegen` 26/26, `--pattern x86` 223/223. With it off the identity tag is neutral to
slightly positive: `binary` 730 → 729 and `fhourstones` 635 → 634 instructions, checksums unchanged.

### Remaining investigation leads

Start from the dashboard, then confirm each miss in isolation before changing the
compiler. The still-open leads are:

- **Vectorization bounds.** Prove typed-buffer bounds and memory dependence for
  conditional-count, max-reduction, and stencil loops (`cd`, `viterbi`, `lbm`,
  `susan`).
- **Loop homes and spill traffic.** Reduce spills across inner loops (`sssp`,
  `mont64`, NYC `pbkdf2`), track available loads by root and byte range
  (`bce-matrix`), and scalar-replace fixed buffers after unrolling (`mont64`).
- **Integer width and allocation.** Narrow provable integer lanes (`bitcount`,
  `intops`, crypto) and reduce managed allocation and operand validation without
  weakening pointer checks (`bigint-oracle`).
- **Measurement boundaries.** Separate startup from kernel cost (`native-oracle`)
  and reuse CFG, dominator, and SCEV results only where invalidation permits
  (`page-rank`, `dce`, `sha256`).
- **Scalar loops after if-conversion.** Two-sided exits now fold to one
  unsigned compare (`nyr_range_check_fold`); the `&&`-inside-one-branch form
  (`x >= 0 && x < C` as a value) is still two compares. Coalesce the
  two-address copy before ALU operations on block-local temporaries, and
  stop spilling short block-local temporaries for "register pressure" with
  free colors (`fhourstones` `won`/`evaluate`, dijkstra argmin).
- **Allocation reuse.** BigInt/BigFloat temporaries are never recycled;
  page faults on fresh memory are a third of `bigint-oracle`. Replacing the locked
  `g_ny_alloc_count` increment in `rt_malloc_impl` with a plain load/store
  changed nothing (26.17 M vs 26.28 M cycles, nine runs), so the allocator's
  atomic is not the cost; rejected and reverted. The generational
  collector in `runtime/gc.c` cannot take these temporaries as is: it is a
  separate opt-in heap with precise roots (`gNyGcTempRoots`), and native
  NYR/LLVM code does not register its live BigInt values as roots, so moving
  BigInt results there needs root maps (or a conservative stack scan) from
  the code generators first.
- **Recursive frames.** `ackermann` (9-run runner, 2026-11-24: NYC 3.5×, NYR
  3.3×, LLVM 2.2×; NYC 630 M instructions vs clang 268 M). NYC's `ack` already
  returns early on `m == 0` and turns the tail call into a loop, but the
  slow path pushes four callee-saved registers where clang pushes one: the
  constant `1` (v9, folded into every use) still gets color 2 (r14) and a dead
  `mov r14d, 1`, the call-argument temporary takes rbx, and both parameters
  are stored to frame homes and reloaded immediately. `segment_color_limit`
  does not restrict these segments, so the callee-saved choice comes from
  copy-affinity hints in `passes/regalloc/regalloc.c`.
- **Loop registers for buffer bases.** `sssp` NYC reloaded `dist`, `ev` and
  `ew` from stack homes on every access because boosted offset temporaries
  outranked them; fixed by `mach_loop_root_boost` (see the 2026-11-23 entry).
  Bases that cross calls still cannot take caller-saved colors
  (`mach_loop_gprs_call_home` only bridges callee-saved ones). The 9-run
  runner put gcbench (1.32×), graph-bfs (1.22×) and word-frequency-scan
  (1.00×) under 2×; their dashboard rows were noise.
- **Vectorizing module-global buffers.** `lbm` NYR 3.8× (NYC 1.24×, which
  vectorizes): the Ny program keeps `a`/`b` in top-level globals that swap
  every step. After global promotion the buffers are a latch swap pair that
  `tbuf_swap_phi_pair` already proves disjoint; the blocker is the inner
  loop's strength-reduced byte-offset IVs (`idx ± 1`, `± W`) that start from
  the outer row offset. Accepting such invariant-start IVs in `affine_value`
  gets past "unsupported loop-carried PHI", but the offset then has no proven
  range (`value_range_under_guards` cannot bound an IV seeded by the outer
  loop), so the load is still refused. Needs a two-level range proof, or
  vectorizing before IV strength reduction.
- **Inlining leaf helpers in loops.** `nyr_should_inline_general` rejects
  `won` and `evaluate` on the 128-block loop pressure limit; LLVM inlines
  both into `search` (`fhourstones`).
- **Vector loops.** Rotate and unroll vector loops with several accumulators,
  keep vector constants resident instead of reloading them, and widen the
  `binary`, `matmul-int`, and `susan` kernels.
- **Inter-block moves.** Split critical edges and coalesce PHIs (`dijkstra`,
  `records`, `heapsort`, `json-parser`); FP scheduling and small-helper
  inlining for `povray`; callee-save shrink-wrapping for `ackermann`.
- **JIT data placement.** `nussinov` runs 1.5× slower in the in-process
  native JIT than as an AOT executable with identical code.
- **Compile time.** Store loop-GPR range starts and ends as `uint32_t`, and
  prune dead standard-library functions before type validation and object
  emission (`pong.ny`, `editor.ny`).

### nested short-circuit if-conversion: rejected at the PHI class gate

Goal: remove the branch diamond that `if (ax < 8 && ay < 8) hits++` leaves in
`cd`'s inner loop, where NYC is the largest remaining runtime miss. Input was
`cd.c` extracted from `etc/labs/tests/bench/**/cd.nshape`; route was
`nyc -O3 -c cd.c -o cd.o`, linked with the host `cc`; reference was
`clang -O3 -march=native`. Both executables returned checksum `387`.

Counters (`perf stat -e instructions:u,cycles:u`, whole process): NYC 80.99 M /
22.20 M, Clang 12.72 M / 3.16 M. The inner `j` loop is 18 instructions per
element in NYC with two conditional branches; Clang emits `cmp`/`setb`/`and`
plus `add` for two elements, so the gap is the un-converted condition, not
memory traffic.

Experiment on `src/code/backend/passes/cfg/if_convert.c`: add `SELECT_I64` to the
speculation whitelist (checking the third operand's availability), accept a
header block that ends `BR_IF target; BR fallthrough` and erase that trailing
branch on rewrite, and add `NYTRIX_IFCONV_TRACE` reject tracing. `cd` and a
minimal two-function probe produced byte-identical objects with and without the
change, so the pass ran and rewrote seven other diamonds in `cd` (the four
`if (ax < 0) ax = -ax` clamps and the modulo) but never reached the target.

Tracing located the gate: the join has exactly one PHI, both arm values pass
`ifc_arm_value_ok`, and `ifc_join_phis` matches both incoming labels, but the
value-class check reports `raw=0` without ever calling `ifc_value_raw`. The join
PHI's `scalar_class` is therefore neither `NYIR_SCALAR_I64_RAW` nor
`NYIR_SCALAR_UNKNOWN`, so the recovery path is skipped; short-circuit flattening
also never fires for this header shape. Rejected and reverted: an unmeasured
relaxation of the class rule could speculate tagged-integer arithmetic, and this
fixture is exactly where tagged increments are not raw adds.

Next lever: decide the class from the arm definitions when the PHI itself is
unclassified (only for a proven integer class), or form the conjunction during
C lowering before SSA so the diamond never exists. Any codegen attempt needs
`NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0`, the `cd` checksum `387`, and the full-report `cd` row for
the before/after.

### partial unrolling: correct, measured, opt-in

Profile first. `cd` (`etc/labs/tests/bench/**/cd.nshape`, C source, `nyc -O3 -c`,
host `cc` link, checksum `387`): `perf record` attributes 98.4% of cycles to
`main`, and the inner `j` loop is 18 instructions per element with two
conditional branches for `if (ax < 8 && ay < 8) hits++`. Clang `-O3
-march=native` spends about 4.5 instructions per element there (`cmp`/`setb`/
`and`/`add`, unrolled two at a time). Whole process: NYC 80.99 M instructions /
22.22 M cycles, Clang 12.72 M / 4.64 M; the gap is the branch and the missing
second copy, not memory traffic.

Implementation: `nyr_loop_unroll_partial` in `passes/loops/loop.c` clones the
whole natural loop once and guards the clone with `iv + 1 < bound`, then
re-joins every header PHI in a merge block that branches back to the original
header. It is fail-closed: one preheader edge, one `cmp.lt iv, bound` guard,
unit step, one latch, one loop exit, no calls or returns in the loop, no
loop-local value escaping except through a header PHI, and at most 64 loop
instructions. The cloned function is verified with `nyr_ir_verify` and only
committed when valid.

Measured result: the transform applies to `cd` and keeps checksum `387`, but it
loses. Retired instructions 80.99 M → 95.07 M (+17%), cycles 22.22 M → 26.56 M.
The disassembly shows why: the doubled body raises pressure enough that the
loop-invariant base pointers spill and are reloaded from `[rbp-0x98]` and
`[rbp-0x90]` on every element (two extra loads), while the guard, induction
update, and merge PHIs cost about what the removed compare and branch saved. A
synthetic single-block dynamic reduction behaved the same way (4.000 G → 4.200 G
instructions). `lbm`, `viterbi`, and `susan` were unchanged (`NYTRIX_NO_UNROLL_PARTIAL`
A/B: ±3 instructions), and their checksums held (`152700`, `3270530`,
`115873346`).

Decision: `NYTRIX_UNROLL_PARTIAL=1` opts in; the default stays rolled. Both the
default and opt-in paths compile and run all 84 `--pattern loop`, 15
`--pattern codegen`, and 11 `--pattern regalloc` fixtures. The retained
opportunity is to unroll only after the allocator can keep the base pointers
resident for the doubled body, or to widen the reduction instead of copying it.

### loop-home allocator: invariant address roots

The unrolled `cd` body spilled the two `calloc` buffers it indexes (`px`, `py`)
and reloaded `[rbp-0x98]`/`[rbp-0x90]` once per element. The loop-home
allocator never considered them: a buffer base is the destination of a CALL and
`other_def` rejects any value whose definition is not a loop-home-safe opcode,
so the pointer could not win a color regardless of how hot it was. The trap is
that `loop_carried` (live across the backedge) is also true for an invariant, so
the carried flag cannot separate a base pointer from a recurrence; a single
definition does, which is what `defs[v] <= 1` tests here.

Change in `mach_allocate_loop_gprs` (`mach/arch/x86/object64.c`):
`loop_root[v]` marks a single-definition value read by a loop body's memory
addressing (`LOAD` src0, `STORE` dst, or an `ADD`/`LEA` whose result feeds one)
whose definition sits no deeper than that use. Call-defined roots bypass
`other_def` (the value arrives in `rax`, so the definition emits one move into
the reserved color and `mach_loop_gprs_call_home` already saves it around
later calls). Roots rank at twice their score, which lifts them over same-depth
temporaries while still losing to hotter carried values. Roots defined by COPY
are excluded from the multiplier: they mirror another vreg, and doubling them
cost `viterbi` 7.4% (57.68 M → 61.96 M) by outranking its DP recurrences;
ranking all roots ahead of everything rather than a bounded boost did the same.

Measured (`NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0`, host `cc` link, best of five `perf stat` runs):
`cd` with the partial unroll goes 95.07 M → 87.07 M instructions (−8.4%) and
26.56 M → 26.20 M cycles (cycles are noisy on this host), checksum `387`, with
zero loads of `[rbp-0x98]`/`[rbp-0x90]` — both bases stay in callee registers
across the doubled body. Default `cd` is unchanged at 80.99 M instructions /
22.18 M cycles, checksum `387`, object byte-identical. `lbm` 50.05 M,
`viterbi` 57.68 M, `susan` 46.26 M: all within ±1 instruction of the previous
build (their objects differ only in register naming where the color order
changed). 84 `--pattern loop`, 15 `--pattern codegen`, and 11 `--pattern
regalloc` fixtures pass on both paths.

The unroll is still opt-in because the rolled build is faster (80.99 M vs
87.07 M): this change removes its documented prerequisite, not its cost. The
remaining gap is the guard, induction update, and merge PHIs, which is where a
reduction widening would act next.

### partial unrolling: guard hoisting

With the bases resident the body was still guarded per element: the clone was
entered from the original latch and re-tested `iv + 1 < bound` inside the loop,
with a merge block re-joining the header PHIs, so each pair cost a second
compare and branch plus one copy per carried value. That kept the unrolled build
behind the rolled one (87.07 M vs 80.99 M instructions).

Restructure: the pair is now the main loop and the original loop is the
remainder. A fresh header takes the preheader edge — directly, or through a new
block when the preheader reaches the header by fallthrough — carries the header
PHIs, and tests `iv + 1 < bound`. Its true edge enters copy A, whose latch
enters copy B, whose latch closes the pair loop with the values copy B produced.
The guard's false edge falls through to the original header, which keeps its own
compare, induction update, and latch, so the last 0..1 iterations run there with
no merge PHIs. The compare that produced the old guard's condition is omitted
from both copies when nothing else reads it, and the bound must be
loop-invariant because the pair header re-tests it. Two bugs surfaced while
building this: the pair PHIs first took copy A's value instead of copy B's, so
the loop advanced by one and discarded copy B's work (145.35 M, checksum still
`387` because only copy A's counter survived), and the pair header's incoming
PHI label must name the fallthrough block, not the preheader.

Measured (`NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0`, host `cc` link, best of five): `cd` with
`NYTRIX_UNROLL_PARTIAL=1` goes 80.99 M → 78.49 M instructions (−3.1%), checksum
`387`, with no loads of either base pointer's home. Cycles 22.17 M → 22.81 M at
the same time, the one number that does not move the same way (best-of-five
still varies by about 1 M on this host), so this is an instruction-count win,
not yet a demonstrated time win. `lbm` 50.05 M, `viterbi` 57.68 M, and `susan`
46.26 M are unchanged. 84 `--pattern loop`, 15 `--pattern codegen`, and 11
`--pattern regalloc` fixtures pass.

The unroll stays opt-in: the gain is 3% of instructions on one fixture with
cycles slightly worse, so the default keeps the rolled loop until the fixture
set shows a consistent win.

### partial unrolling: why cycles do not track the instruction drop

`cd` with the guard-hoisted unroll retires 78.49 M instructions against
80.99 M rolled, but 22.81 M cycles against 22.17 M. The counters say where that
goes: L1-dcache loads 8.54 M → 9.11 M (+578 k), branch misses 82.6 k → 100.0 k
(+21%), branches 12.115 M → 12.255 M. Loads dominate, and 578 k is exactly the
per-entry cost of this loop: the inner `j` loop is entered 40 k times (200 steps
× 200 rows), and the unrolled build spills and reloads the row-invariant
`px[i]`, `py[i]`, and `iv + 1` at the pair entry — `mov [rbp-0x210],rax`
followed seven instructions later by `mov rax,[rbp-0x210]` — about fifteen
memory operations per entry. The pair body itself is fine: 47 instructions per
two elements against 23 per element rolled, with both array bases resident.

The traffic is capacity, not legality. The pair loop holds the induction
variable, its guard value, the counter, both array bases, and both row values
live at once, so the row values lose the colour contest and fall back to their
homes. Giving row-invariant values (single-definition, defined inside an outer
loop, read deeper) the loop-home root treatment changed nothing on `cd`, rolled
or unrolled, so that lever was reverted instead of landing unmeasured. Closing
this needs the interval-splitting item already recorded for `sssp`: lend a
colour to the inner loop instead of holding it over the whole outer body.

Net: on `cd` the unroll trades 2.5 M instructions for 0.64 M cycles. It stays
opt-in, and the rolled default is byte-identical to the previously verified
build on `cd`, `lbm`, `viterbi`, and `susan`.

### Spectral checksum and bigint-oracle baseline

On x86-64 (AMD EPYC 7642), the dirty release tree based on `ce2f0f8d`
produced spectral checksum `1274209` at NYC `-O3`. GCC 16.2.1
(`-O3 -march=native`) produced `1287401`. Both reported the spectral norm
`1.274209131289`. Disabling `if_convert` restored the integer checksum.
The final NYIR showed the false edge of the second condition losing its
distinct PHI input when two jump-only paths to the loop latch were merged.
After guarding that transformation, all four routes agreed on `1287401`.

The cold, cache-disabled `ny-test --bench` runs used one warm-up and three
measured samples.
Spectral times its kernel internally. The NYR median was 69 µs (68–70 µs),
LLVM was 107 µs, Host C was 26 µs, and NYC was 76 µs. NYR/C was 2.67× and
NYC/C was 2.92×. GCC's inner products use packed `vdivpd` and NYC's use
scalar `vdivsd`. Before the fix, the focused run rejected the row on
checksum parity, so its timings do not establish a runtime change.

Bigint-oracle produced checksum `1968365` with medians of 27.0 ms NYR
(26.7–27.7 ms), 27.1 ms LLVM, 3.70 ms Host C, and 3.71 ms NYC. NYR and LLVM
run Nytrix's managed immutable arithmetic, while the C assembly calls GMP
with reusable output objects. An exploratory hardware profile sampled
allocation, pointer validation, and BigInt and BigFloat division in the NYR
executable. Unresolved samples prevent assigning reliable percentages.

### Viterbi vector recurrence

On the same x86-64 host and release tree, NYC used `-O3 -c` and GCC 16.2.1
used `-O3 -march=native`. The NYC object previously reloaded and stored
the running vector maximum around every `vblendvpd` in the inner loop. The
vector latch coalescer now recognizes the instruction's read-before-write
semantics and assigns the old and updated maximum to the same YMM register.
The loop no longer reloads or stores the maximum on every iteration.

One warm-up and 15 interleaved, CPU-pinned samples timed the fixture's
internal kernel. Host C took 0.926 ms (0.921–0.956 ms). NYC went from
2.623 ms (2.570–2.858 ms) to 1.469 ms (1.440–1.494 ms), or 2.83× to
1.59× Host C. NYR used `-O3 --native-backend x86_64` and took 2.871 ms
(2.833–2.962 ms) before and 2.858 ms (2.834–2.964 ms) after the change.
All executions produced checksum `3270530`. The cold `ny-test --bench`
run with `NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0` and `NYTRIX_BENCH_CC=cc` confirmed
1.57× for NYC and 2.92× for NYR. NYR's hot loop remains scalar. The
vectorizer rejects the loop-varying signed remainder before a later pass
reduces it to multiply and shift, and the retry rejects a loop-carried PHI.

### Compile-time and runtime waves before the post-inline fix

Host AMD EPYC 7642, Host C `clang 23.1.5 -O3`, NYR and NYC
`-O3 --native-backend x86_64`, all with `NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0`. A full
`./make bench` run passed 110 of 110 fixtures with 109 comparable rows, no
mismatches, no noisy rows, and all seven per-class gates. For C reference
times of at least 1 ms (`n=62`), NYR/C was 1.14×, LLVM/C 0.96×, and NYR/LLVM
1.19×. Over all valid rows (`n=109`) the ratios were 1.26×, 1.07×, and 1.18×.

Runtime changes and their measured effect on the NYR route:

- `xz` 4.24 ms to 1.84–1.94 ms. If-conversion keeps the `go = 0` exit flag,
  and jump threading collapses one-incoming PHIs and threads fallthrough edges.
- `repeat` from about 5.5× to 0.23–0.37× Host C (21.6 ms against LLVM's
  22.6 ms). Allocations of at least 2 MiB request `MADV_HUGEPAGE`, and the
  repeat pattern buffer is 32 KiB.
- `fhourstones` 3.61 ms to 2.29–2.38 ms. If-conversion gained a select arm
  operation and a depth-64 raw-PHI proof; the encoder keeps 64-bit literals.
- `radix-tree` 6.23–6.30 ms on NYR and NYC (1.12× LLVM) from a resident-base
  SIB addressing fast path.
- `nussinov` 4.11 ms to 3.82 ms, `bigint-oracle` 32.2 ms to 11.0–17.2 ms,
  and `mandel` 335 µs to 137 µs.

Cold compile time with `ny-full -O3 --native-backend x86_64 --stats`:

| Target | Before | After (quiet / loaded) | Type validation | Emit obj |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `etc/labs/pong.ny` | 14.78 s | 8.50–9.20 s | 3.40 s → 1.05–1.10 s | 8.76 s → 5.18–5.41 s |
| `etc/labs/editor.ny` | 33.92 s | 21.00–28.03 s | 6.67 s → 2.38–2.57 s | 23.58 s → 15.44–19.87 s |

The compile-time changes: demand-driven standard-library HM scheme inference
(`NYTRIX_HM_EAGER_STD=1` restores eager inference), constant-time alias,
layout, attached-method, module, export, and module-variable indexes, a
negative export-alias cache, a file-line cache for FFI return types, a
per-function local hash table in parallel fork lowering, CFG and range
scratch reuse, lazy loop depths in the general inliner, and string
deduplication when cloning NYIR functions. The same batch made the parallel
post-inline sweep skip functions whose inlining did not change; the next
entry measures what that cost at runtime.

`./make test` passed 2,548 of 2,548 fixtures. `ctest` passed 61 of 63; the
two failures predate the batch (`ny-stdlib-debug-path` without
`NYTRIX_STD_CACHE`, and `nytrix-addr-probe-device` reading the second
`[vvar]` page).

### Post-inline parity, dead NOP blocks, and affine IV reduction

Same host and flags as the previous entry. Each change was measured on its
own with the switch noted, then together in a full run.

- **Post-inline parity.** The parallel NYIR build skipped post-inline
  optimization for functions whose inlining did not change; the sequential
  build optimizes every function a second time. That second O3 run is what
  fully unrolls and if-converts leaf helpers such as `fhourstones`'s
  `evaluate`. The parallel route optimizes every function again and now
  emits code identical to `NYTRIX_SERIAL_NYIR_BUILD=1`. In a whole-suite AOT
  A/B the restored run took 0.43× the time on `radix-tree`, 0.49× on
  `records`, 0.54× on `list-any-append`, 0.59× on `recursive-fibonacci`, and
  0.83× on `fhourstones`. Rows that looked slower (`string`, `linpack`) were
  faster on a 15-run recheck. `pong.ny` compiled in 9.2–9.3 s either way.
- **Dead NOP blocks.** After an unconditional `br` or `ret`, a label-less
  block holding only NOPs fell through into the next label as an extra
  predecessor. In NYC's `cd`, jump threading left `br→L49; nop×3; L49: br→L43`
  on the false edge of `hits += 1`, so the triangle's bridge had two
  predecessors and if-conversion declined it. The CFG builder now gives such
  blocks no successor. `cd` NYC went from 6.68 ms to 1.84 ms (Host C 0.90 ms)
  because its pair loop vectorizes. Merging the NOPs into the terminating
  block instead broke passes that refill NOP slots.
- **Affine induction-variable reduction.** `ivelim` now reduces a multiply
  or constant shift whose operand is affine in a basic IV, `(a*iv + b) * C`
  with `b` loop-invariant, to a new IV. The init is computed in the
  preheader, so non-constant inits such as `k = i + 1` qualify. The outermost
  product is reduced first and dead products are removed between rounds.
  `NYTRIX_NO_AFFINE_SR=1` disables it. `nussinov`'s divisibility test and row
  addressing become additions. Its AOT executable went from 4.49 ms to
  2.83 ms (Host C 3.2 ms); the bench's in-process JIT route stayed near
  4.3 ms with the same transforms, which points at data placement rather than
  code.
- **Direct `setcc`.** A compare whose result has a register now writes
  `setcc r8; movzx r32, r8` into that register instead of going through
  `rax`. `NYTRIX_NO_SETCC_DIRECT=1` disables it. `fhourstones` NYR went from
  2.58 ms to 2.19 ms.
- The in-memory JIT resolves each relocation symbol once and shares far-call
  stubs per target. Cache publication renames the finished file even when
  another process holds the cache directory lock, instead of discarding it.

The full `./make bench` run with all of these passed 110 of 110 fixtures,
no mismatches, and all seven gates. For C reference times of at least 1 ms
the ratios were NYR/C 1.05× and NYR/LLVM 1.14×. The full fixture tree passed
2,548 of 2,548, and `./make check` passed.

### Collector primitive typing for operator reachability (reverted)

The native reachability collector cannot type many stdlib operands, such as
`count * 10` with `def count = load64_i(...)`, so it collects every impl
body declaring that operator. `recursive-fibonacci` built 336 functions,
mostly from `core.math.crypto`, `core.math.big`, and `core.math.ring`.
Typing int and f64 arithmetic, self-referential locals, and int-returning
intrinsics in the collector cut that to 287. Lowering, however, compiles
untyped arithmetic to dynamic dispatch, which reaches bigint operators at
runtime. The collector dropped `core.math.big.bigint.le`, and 19 bench
fixtures failed in the native JIT with an unresolved symbol. The change was
reverted. Reducing this over-collection requires the collector to reproduce
lowering's typing exactly, not to know more than lowering does.

### Shared-join if-conversion, address folding, unsigned compares, loop GVN

Host EPYC 7642 (Zen 2), under concurrent load (a NYC kernel build ran
for part of the work), so the evidence below is user-mode instruction counts
(`perf stat -e instructions:u,branch-misses:u`, whole process), which are
load-independent. Routes: NYC `nyc -O3 -c` linked with `cc`; NYR AOT
`ny -O3 --native-only --run=none`; Host C `clang -O3 -march=native`. Baseline
is HEAD b2c14da7 built in a scratch tree; candidate is that tree plus the
changes below. `NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0 NYTRIX_JIT_CACHE=0`. Every checksum matched
the baseline and C.

| Fixture | Route | C insns | Before | After | Branch misses before → after |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| fhourstones | NYC | 7.41 M | 20.75 M | 25.31 M | 91.8 k → 31.8 k (C 33.1 k) |
| dijkstra | NYC | 90.7 M | 244.6 M | 282.5 M | 423 k → 393 k |
| escape-loop | NYC | 15.2 M | 94.2 M | 19.1 M | — |
| JSON number scan (`bench/string`) | NYC | 178.8 M | 362.3 M | 212.4 M | — |
| fhourstones | NYR | — | 24.70 M | 19.91 M | — |
| JSON number scan (`bench/string`) | NYR | 178.8 M | 259.1 M | 152.3 M | — |
| dijkstra | NYR | — | 306.1 M | 334.6 M | 451 k → 427 k |
| cd | NYR | — | 27.28 M | 27.10 M | unchanged |

Instruction counts rise where branches become selects (fhourstones and
dijkstra NYC): on a quiet moment fhourstones NYC measured 7.65 M → 6.6–6.9 M
cycles and dijkstra NYC 116 M → 90 M cycles; escape-loop NYC went from about
8.4 ms to 4.6 ms (clang 4.1 ms). Re-measure all of these with `./make bench`
on a quiet host before quoting ratios.

Mechanisms, each with its switch:

- **Join splitting before if-conversion** (`cfg/if_convert.c`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_IFCONV_SPLIT_JOIN=1`). `if (a) x+=k; else if (b) x-=k;` and
  `if (!done[v] && dist[v] < bd)` thread their inner join into the outer one,
  leaving a three-predecessor PHI the diamond matcher rejects. The pass gives
  the inner arms their own join (PHIs only where the arm values differ; an
  empty bridge shared with the outer edge is first unshared), converts the
  inner diamond, removes dead arm blocks with DCE, threads the now-empty
  forwarder, and converts the outer diamond. It runs only in the final
  if-conversion (`nyr_ifconv_late`): splitting before the vectorizer hid
  `cd`'s nested conditional count from it (NYR cd went 27 M → 109 M until
  gated). A trailing `br` after `br.if` forms its own CFG block; the arm
  check looks through it.
- **Late address reassociation** (`advanced.c` `nyr_addr_reassoc`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_ADDR_REASSOC=1`). Groups of `B + ((x + C) << K)` share one
  `B + (x << K)`; each access becomes `[base + disp]`. Placing one shared base
  matters: `ilp_reassociate` re-splits a single-use `(B + s) + C` into
  `(B + C) + s`.
- **Encoder displacement fold** (`object64.c` `mach_disp_add_mem`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_DISP_FOLD=1`): an adjacent single-use `base + const` feeding a
  scalar load or store is emitted as `[reg + disp]`; `mach_emit_mem` now adds
  the SIB byte an RSP/R12 base needs with a displacement. A constant is no
  longer taken as a SIB index (it was materialized in R10 per access).
- **Register select** (`NYTRIX_NO_SELECT_REG=1`): a select whose condition,
  arms and result are resident is `test; mov; cmov` instead of staging
  through RAX/R10 (six instructions).
- **Non-adjacent `shl 32`/`sar 32` → `movsxd`** (`lower/machine.c`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_SEXT_FAR=1`): if-conversion interleaves the shift pairs of C
  `int` updates.
- **Dead allocation elimination** (`nyr_dead_alloc_elim`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_DEAD_ALLOC=1`): a libc `malloc` (NYC marks eligible calls with
  `NYIR_INST_F_LIBC_ALLOC`: not `-fno-builtin`, not defined or renamed in the
  unit) whose pointer is only stored through, null-tested and freed is
  removed, as LLVM does. escape-loop no longer calls malloc/free.
- **Loop GVN for shifts and bit operations** (`scalar/gvn_pre.c`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_GVN_LOOP_BITOPS=1`): the in-loop whitelist and the cross-block
  operand check now include SHL/SAR/AND/OR/XOR, and a load replaced by load
  PRE forwards its identity, so repeated `s[i]` sign extensions merge.
- **Unsigned compares on x86-64** (`lower/machine.c`
  `mach_fuse_unsigned_cmp`, `NYTRIX_NO_UNSIGNED_CMP=1`): NYC lowers
  `a <u b` to `(a ^ MIN) <s (b ^ MIN)`; machine IR now compares the originals
  with B/BE/A/AE (setcc/jcc tables extended, verifier accepts them).
- **Range-check fold** (`nyr_range_check_fold`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_RANGE_CHECK_FOLD=1`): `if (c < L) goto X; if (c > H) goto X;`
  and Ny's single `br.if (c < L || c > H)` become one `c - L >u H - L` test
  (the JSON digit scan: `lea; cmp; ja`; NYR about 19 ms to 14 ms, C 13 ms).
  It and the unsigned-compare fuse act on the compared 64-bit words, so both
  apply to NYR as well as NYC.
- **Recursive frames** (`object64.c`): a literal decolored by
  `mach_decolor_literals` is no longer handed a color again by the
  local-spill recolor (`NYTRIX_NO_LITERAL_RECOLOR_SKIP=1`), and the
  direct-parameter window steps over immediate copies ahead of the parameter
  loads (`NYTRIX_NO_PARAM_PREFIX_CONST=1`). ackermann NYC: 630.5 M to
  520.5 M instructions, 188 M to 154 M cycles (clang 268 M / 87 M): no dead
  `mov r14d, 1` and r14 push/pop, parameters moved from rdi/rsi instead of
  stored and reloaded. NYR 598.9 M to 567.5 M. Making call-argument
  temporaries prefer caller-saved colors removed the remaining rbx push
  (520 M to 489 M instructions) but saved no cycles and was neutral on six
  other C benchmarks, so it was not kept.
- **Loop-register ranking** (`object64.c` `mach_loop_root_boost`,
  `NYTRIX_LOOP_ROOT_BOOST_ARITH=1` restores the old rule): the 2× rank boost
  for address roots no longer applies to ADD/SUB/LEA-defined roots. Those are
  per-iteration offset temporaries (`k*8 + 16`); boosting them (1536 to 3072)
  displaced `sssp`'s three buffer bases (COPY roots, 2560/2048), which then
  reloaded twelve times per iteration. NYC cycles, best of seven: sssp
  23.28 M to 13.09 M (C 9.7 M), graph-bfs 67.1 M to 59.0 M, qrsyndrome
  11.6 M to 10.1 M, thread-ring 61.5 M to 58.9 M; deltablue +3%, sor +3%,
  permute +1% (host load about 24). Every C benchmark kept its checksum. NYR:
  sssp 13.71 M to 12.64 M cycles, dijkstra +2%; viterbi, cd, lbm,
  fhourstones, mont64, susan and the JSON scan within ±1 instruction.
- **GVN disjointness for stack objects** (`scalar/gvn_pre.c`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_GVN_ALLOCA_BOUNDS=1`): two different ALLOCA roots were disjoint
  only when both offsets were zero; accesses that each lie inside their own
  alloca now count as disjoint too. mont64's promoted limb buffers (a 48-byte
  `tbuf` and three 32-byte limb arrays) reloaded every `a[j]`, `n[j]` after
  each `tbuf[j]` store. NYR mont64: 13.92 M to 12.36 M cycles (instructions
  +0.9%), checksum unchanged. NYC mont64 +3% cycles (10.91 M to 11.24 M,
  longer live ranges in its unrolled body); every other C benchmark has the
  same checksum and instructions within 0.5% (thread-ring's objects are
  byte-identical; its count is spin noise); NYR viterbi, cd, lbm, dijkstra,
  fhourstones, susan, sssp, graph-bfs, the JSON scan, bitcount and nussinov
  within ±0.1%.
- **phiopt** now sinks the COPYs it makes from PHIs below the block's
  remaining PHIs; `--nyr-verify` previously failed on that in
  `editor-pane-bounds` and `ui-icon-cache` at HEAD.

Verification: the full fixture tree passed 2548/2548 with every change
(the one failure in the final run was `ny-doc-search-ny-discovery`, whose
query ranking this entry's wording had disturbed; reworded), the
focused intermediate/math/execution/values/control-flow set passed, and a
differential run of llvm-test-suite `SingleSource` (Benchmarks, UnitTests,
Regression; 611 programs NYC can compile) against the baseline NYC and GCC
found no output change. A full-tree run with per-pass verification found only
the HEAD phiopt issue (fixed) and the intended division-by-zero fixture.
A second `./make bench` after the NYR range fold also passed every gate
(NYR geomean 1.21×, NYC 1.28×, NYC 97/109 within 2×); its records and
dict-set rows are timing noise (NYC instructions base 127.7 M / 106.1 M vs
candidate 125.3 M / 106.2 M; the C reference ran 47 ms standalone against
22.5 ms in the report). The full tree then passed 2548/2548. After the
recursive-frame changes the full tree passed again (one UI replay probe
failed under load and passed 8/8 alone), the C differential sweep was
unchanged, and a `./make bench` on a quiet host (load below 8) passed every
gate: NYR 1.22×, LLVM 1.01×, NYC 1.25× geomean; NYC 104/109 and NYR 93/109
within 2× of C. An earlier run under a concurrent kernel build failed
the string NYR/LLVM gate on `append-scaling` (2.61×); nine-run timing gave
NYR 1.98 ms against 2.13 ms at HEAD and 0.24× of LLVM, and the quiet rerun
measured 0.96×. With the loop-register ranking change the full tree passed 2548/2548,
the C differential sweep was unchanged, and `./make bench`
(`bench-20261124-*` after 03:48) passed every gate; load rose to about 15
during that run, and its dashboard rows for bitcount, graph-bfs, prime,
sieve and radix-tree are noise: their AOT instruction counts equal HEAD's
(bitcount NYR 45.08 M both), and graph-bfs is faster than HEAD in cycles
(NYC 56.5 M to 52.9 M, NYR 48.3 M to 42.9 M, best of seven). With the GVN alloca
change the full tree passed 2548/2548 and the C differential sweep was
unchanged (two more programs matched GCC). Its `./make bench` started at load
7 but ended at load 25 under concurrent builds and failed the string
NYR/LLVM gate on `append-scaling` again (2.02×); nine-run timing on the same
host gave NYR 1.89 ms (0.24× of LLVM) against 1.77 ms at HEAD, and records
NYR 41.1 ms against 40.4 ms, so the gate tripped on load, not on code. A full gated
`ny-test --bench` with the frozen candidate binaries (`./make bench` could not
build `build/release` while a concurrent `emitter.c` edit was broken)
passed every class but concurrency, which missed by 0.01 (NYR/LLVM 1.45×,
limit 1.44×; load fell from 15 to 9 during the run). Nine-run timing of that
class matched HEAD: calls 95 µs both, call-chain 503 vs 506 µs, thread-ring
3.42 vs 3.35 ms, richards 1.19 vs 1.18 ms (NYR/LLVM 1.02× for the class); the
swing was LLVM's thread-ring time (3.36 vs 3.75 ms). Once the tree built again,
`./make bench` (`bench-20261124-050205-161731`) passed every per-class gate
with all checksums correct, at load 21 to 30.
`./make bench` (report `bench-20261123-233709-1786163`) passed every
per-class gate with all checksums correct. Rows that moved against history
in that single run (NYR append-scaling, bitcount, the JSON scan, sieve, stream,
dict-set, list-dynamic) have identical AOT instruction counts before and
after (for example bitcount 45.08 M both), so they are run-to-run noise.

Not fixed, measured: **bigint-oracle** (NYR 12 ms vs GMP 4.2 ms) spends a
third of its time faulting in fresh pages. Every BigInt/BigFloat result is a
new managed allocation that is never reused; on this host a 4 KiB fault costs
about 2.2 µs (MAP_POPULATE does not help; THP falls back because memory is
fragmented). Closing it needs temporaries to be recycled, not faster
arithmetic. Remaining fhourstones NYC cost is two block-local temporaries per
cell spilled for "register pressure" with seven colors in use.


### Stack-buffer addressing and bench worker count

Host EPYC 7642 (Zen 2). Evidence is whole-process `instructions:u` from AOT
executables (`ny -O3 --native-backend x86_64 --run=none`,
`NYTRIX_AOT_CACHE=0 NYTRIX_JIT_CACHE=0`), both switches below off vs on, over
all 110 bench fixtures; every checksum matched.

- **Address-only copy fold** (`passes/scalar/copy_prop.c`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_ADDR_COPY_FOLD=1`). `mut ptr a = i64buf_new(N)` lowers to an
  `alloca` plus `mov v0 [abi=ptr]` retags, one per access site. The
  scalar-class contract kept each copy, and each took a home reloaded on every
  access (cmov-sort's insertion loop reloaded two bases per iteration). A
  class-contract COPY of an `alloca` whose result only forms load/store
  addresses, directly or through one add, now folds.
- **Frame SIB** (`mach/arch/x86/object64.c` `mach_frame_sib_pending`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_FRAME_ADD_SIB=1`). An elided `add frame_addr, index` feeding the
  adjacent scalar load or store is emitted as `[rbp + index*scale + slot]`
  instead of `lea rax,[rbp-slot]` per access. Vector accesses keep the RAX
  form (they consume pending SIB without a displacement).

| Fixture | NYR insns before | After |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| cmov-sort (inner loop 12 → 9 insns) | 12.76 M | 9.71 M |
| havlak | 14.48 M | 12.48 M |
| matmult-int | 9.75 M | 8.88 M |
| deltablue | 332.8 M | 315.6 M |
| stringsearch | 8.51 M | 8.11 M |
| viterbi | 12.12 M | 11.56 M |
| linpack | 2.37 M | 2.27 M |

No fixture rose by more than 0.1%. NYC records is unchanged by either switch
(125.39 M instructions both).

**Bench noise.** `./make bench` passed `--jobs $(nproc)` (96; the RAM guard
cut it to about 52), overriding the runner's half-physical-cores default, so
about 50 fixtures timed microsecond kernels at once on SMT siblings. Across
eight same-day reports one fixture swung from 1.34× to 8.89× (NYR bitcount)
and records from 0.15× to 6.44×. With the runner's default (24 workers),
`./make bench` (`bench-20261124-084206-700193`, load about 7) passed every
per-class gate. Median per-fixture noise fell from 5.3% to 2.0%, budget misses
from 2 to 0, and rows at or above 2× of C from 18 NYR / 12 NYC to 8 / 7
(overall NYR/C 1.15×, NYR/LLVM 1.20×). `NYTRIX_BENCH_JOBS` and `-j` still
override.

Still open after this run, each confirmed with nine-run minimums:

- **records NYC** is 4.1× C (59.4 ms vs 14.6 ms); NYR is 1.36×.
- **cmov-sort NYR** still carries three IVs for `j`, `j*8` and `(j+1)*8`
  where C keeps one. Merging them would take the inner loop from 9 to about
  6 instructions.
- **bigint-oracle** has no new findings: the lead under "Allocation reuse"
  still applies. `rt_malloc_impl` plus `rt_heap_ptr_registry_has` are 30% of
  user instructions, and about 1,580 page faults per run take half the wall
  time.
- **intops** at 3.1× NYR in the dashboard is noise (nine-run AOT 1.35× of C,
  unchanged instructions). Kernels under about 20 µs (revcomp 2 µs, page-rank
  5 µs, matrix 7 µs, currying 16 µs) stay near the timer floor; treat their
  ratios as indicative only.


### IV merge, versioned store promotion, frame folds, branch split

Host EPYC 7642 (Zen 2). Evidence is whole-process `instructions:u` of AOT
executables, NYR (`ny -O3 --native-backend x86_64 --run=none`) and NYC
(`nyc -O3 -c`, linked with clang), caches off, each new switch off vs on,
over all 110 bench fixtures; no checksum changed. Wall-clock bench numbers
from this session are not quoted: a concurrent NYC kernel build kept the host
at load 40-55.

- **IV merge** (`passes/loops/iv_merge.c`, `NYTRIX_NO_IV_MERGE=1`). Strength
  reduction gives each address stream its own IV; header PHIs with the same
  constant step now share one, with `B + p2` rebased onto a preheader
  `B + (i2 - i1)`. With a non-constant difference it merges only when every
  use rebases (xz NYR's short match loops still pay +2.9% instructions,
  +0.7% time, for the preheader setup).
- **Base reassociation** (`passes/scalar/addr_base.c`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_ADDR_BASE_REASSOC=1`): four or more adjacent `(B + C_k) + x`
  accesses in one block share `B + x` and fold `C_k` as displacements
  (revcomp's unrolled lane loop kept 16 bases and spilled 27). Two or three
  members, or a frame base, are left alone: at 2-3 members expression-tree
  lost 28% in cycles to allocation perturbation.
- **Versioned store promotion** (`passes/loops/store_promote.c`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_STORE_PROMOTE=1`): a two-block loop that read-modify-writes one
  invariant cell while loading `q + iv` streams gets a copy that carries the
  cell in a PHI, entered only when `init < limit` and no stream's
  `[q + init, q + limit + 8)` overlaps the cell. page-rank NYR 18.5 -> 7.4 us,
  NYC 20.2 -> 6.7 us (C 4.8 us); copy roots make NYR's two retag copies of
  one pointer one cell.
- **Frame folds** (`object64.c`, `NYTRIX_NO_FRAME_FOLD_ANY=1`):
  single-definition frame LEAs fold from any block, `frame + c` folds with an
  addend, and a folded frame address is a memory operand `[rbp + disp]`.
  records NYC's compound-literal fill loop went 80 -> 71 instructions.
- **Branch split** (`passes/cfg/and_branch_split.c`,
  `NYTRIX_NO_AND_BRANCH_SPLIT=1`): loop tests `c1 && c2` (including
  if-conversion's `(cmp ^ 1) & c2`) become two compare-and-branch pairs.
- **`1 & cmp`** (`peephole.c`, `NYTRIX_NO_CMP_AND1=1`) folds only when the
  result feeds branches; folding it everywhere broke a tagged-bool decode
  match and cost nussinov NYR +40%.
- **Division registers** (`NYTRIX_NO_DIV_DIRECT=1`): the magic-division
  sequence takes a resident dividend straight into R10 and moves the quotient
  from RDX to its register.
- Fixed: ISLE identity rewrites (`lower/isle.c`) turned operands into IMM in
  place and kept their register class; the machine verifier rejected the
  function and the encoder silently fell back to all-stack code (NYC
  matmul-int ran 5x the instructions when IV merge exposed it).

| Fixture | Route | Before | After |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: |
| lbm | NYR | 21.59 M | 16.65 M |
| susan | NYR / NYC | 23.65 / 24.94 M | 18.56 / 21.69 M |
| jacobi-2d | NYR / NYC | 11.39 / 12.11 M | 9.58 / 10.79 M |
| sssp | NYR / NYC | 51.88 / 56.00 M | 44.55 / 47.63 M |

Full suite 2548/2548 after each step.

Still open:

- **bigint-oracle** NYR is allocation-bound (see "Allocation reuse").
- **ackermann** and **call-chain** are call-frame cost (see "Recursive
  frames").
- **records** NYC: wall time is page faults (14,091 per run on both C and
  NYC; C itself ranged 12.8-58 ms between runs). The remaining fill-loop cost
  is the compound-literal temporary copied into the array element; forwarding
  the temporary's stores into the destination would remove it.
- **mandel** and **native-oracle**: Ny is timed by the JIT run phase and C by
  the kernel, so microsecond kernels compare different boundaries.

</details>
